        "anchor": "stmt-lem-h-certified-variation",
        "exact_quote": "For the full endpoint outputs $Z_{G,s}=\\overline L_{G,s}\\mathcal B_sB_s$ just defined, let $\\mathcal J$ be a common finite collection of depth intervals $[r,t]$ with edge-overlap multiplicity at most $a$. For each $G,[r,t]$, let $E_{G,r,t}$ be a union of $t$-cells having a downstream actual-use certificate for $G$. Then $$\\sum_{G,[r,t]\\in\\mathcal J}\n   \\|\\mathbf1_{E_{G,r,t}}(Z_{G,t}-Z_{G,r})\\|_2^2\n       \\le aG_0|R|.\n \\label{eq:h-certified-variation}$$ For one shared output $Z_s^{\\rm sh}$ in the stated source class, let $E_{r,t}$ be the union of the required $t$-cells across all its uses. Then $$\\sum_{[r,t]\\in\\mathcal J}\n   \\|\\mathbf1_{E_{r,t}}(Z_t^{\\rm sh}-Z_r^{\\rm sh})\\|_2^2\n       \\le aG_0|R|.$$ This shared assertion has no group sum or group certificate. All outputs before the attempt root are zero.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Averaging profile** ([source](#stmt-def-averaging-profile)): An averaging profile is a real function $\varphi\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R;\mathbb R)$ such that, for some $G\geq1$, $$\\|\varphi^{(n)}\\|_\infty \leq\bigl(G(n+2)\bigr)^{G(n+2)} \qquad(n=0,1,2,\ldots), \label{eq:profile-derivatives}$$ and $$\int_{\mathbb R}\varphi(t)\,dt=1,\qquad \int_{\mathbb R}t^j\varphi(t)\,dt=0\quad(1\leq j\leq3). \label{eq:profile-moments}$$ The function may have either sign. For $a>0$, write $\varphi_a(t)=a^{-1}\varphi(t/a)$.
- **Admissible local kernels and associated local forms** ([source](#stmt-def-transfer-local-kernels)): Fix $c_0>0$ and $C_0\geq1$. For an interval $I$ of length $r=\|I\|$, a kernel $w_I\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^2;\mathbb C)$ is admissible if the following hold. 1. For every $(x,t)\in\operatorname{supp}w_I$ and $j\in\{0,1,2,3\}$, $x+jt\in I$ and $\operatorname{dist}(x+jt,\partial I)\geq c_0r$. 2. For every $v\in\mathbb R$ and $j=0,1,2,3$, $$\int_{\mathbb R}w_I(v-jt,t)\,dt=0.$$ 3. For all nonnegative integers $p,q$, with $n=p+q$, $$\\|\partial_x^p\partial_t^q w_I\\|_\infty \leq r^{-n}\bigl(C_0(n+2)\bigr)^{C_0(n+2)}.$$ For measurable scalar functions $z_j:\mathbb R\to\mathbb C$, define the associated local form, whenever its integral is finite, by $$H_I(z_0,z_1,z_2,z_3) =r^{-2}\int_{\mathbb R^2}w_I(x,t) \prod_{j=0}^3z_j(x+jt)\,dx\,dt.$$ For a Hilbert-valued argument with a distinguished physical scalar coordinate, the form uses that coordinate. We write $\\|z\\|_{p,I}=(\|I\|^{-1}\int_I\\|z\\|^p)^{1/p}$ for the normalized interval norm, with the usual essential supremum when $p=\infty$. Norms without an interval subscript use ordinary Lebesgue measure.
- **Triple ergodic average** ([source](#eq:main-average)): For bounded measurable functions $f_1,f_2,f_3:X\to\mathbb C$, put $$A_N(f_1,f_2,f_3)(x) =\frac1N\sum_{n=1}^N f_1(T^n x)f_2(T^{2n}x)f_3(T^{3n}x),\qquad N\ge1. \label{eq:main-average}$$
- **Mixing** ([source](#eq:mixing)): The transformation is *mixing* if, for every $A,B\in\mathcal F$, $$\mu(A\cap T^{-m}B)\longrightarrow\mu(A)\mu(B) \quad\text{as }\|m\|\to\infty,\quad m\in\mathbb Z. \label{eq:mixing}$$
- **Block size and internal variation** ([source](#pm-h-8)): $$ For a Hilbert-valued argument with a distinguished physical scalar coordinate, the form uses that coordinate. We write $\\|z\\|_{p,I}=(\|I\|^{-1}\int_I\\|z\\|^p)^{1/p}$ for the normalized interval norm, with the usual essential supremum when $p=\infty$. Norms without an interval subscript use ordinary Lebesgue measure. </div> For a nonempty consecutive integer block $B=\{a,\ldots,b\}$ and a sequence of complex functions $h_s$ on any common domain, write $$\mathcal V_B(h)(y) =\max_{s\in B}\|h_s(y)\| +\sum_{s=a}^{b-1}\|h_{s+1}(y)-h_s(y)\|.$$ The sum is zero for a singleton block.
- **Normalized Hilbert-valued depth schedule** ([source](#eq:row-input-normalization)): For strongly measurable functions $F_s:R\to\mathcal H$ into a Hilbert space, the block normalization will mean $$\max_{s\in\mathsf S_b}\\|F_s(y)\\|_{\mathcal H} +\sum_{\substack{s,s+1\in\mathsf S_b}} \\|F_{s+1}(y)-F_s(y)\\|_{\mathcal H} \le W^{-1/2} \quad (1\le b\le W) \label{eq:row-input-normalization}$$
- **Persistent private columns** ([source](#eq:row-catalog-budget)): In $$\mathcal H=\mathcal H_0\oplus\ell^2(\mathcal V)$$ define, once and for all, $$\Phi_v(y)=(\phi_v(y),\gamma_v\mathbf e_v),\qquad y\in J_v.$$ The direction $\mathbf e_v$ is private to $v$, including when two physical functions coincide.
- **Available catalog labels** ([source](#eq:row-catalog-budget)): On a descendant cell $Q$ the available labels are $$\mathcal V(Q)=\{v\in\mathcal V:Q\subset J_v\}.$$
- **Edge overlap of depth intervals** ([source](#stmt-lem-depth-input-size)): Its edge overlap is $$\mathfrak m= \max_{s_-\le j<s_+} \#\{[r,t]\in\mathcal J:r\le j<t\},$$ where a maximum over an empty set is zero.
- **Admissible opposing sequences** ([source](#pm-h-20)): For a dyadic $K\subseteq J'$ and a selected finite use collection $\mathcal U\subseteq\mathcal I$ below $K$, call an opposing sequence $z_{e,I}$ admissible on $K$ if $$z_{e,I}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{m_e}c_{e,\ell}V_{e,I}^{\ell},\qquad m_e\in\{0,1,2,3\},\qquad \|c_{e,\ell}\|\le1,$$ where the scalar coefficients $c_{e,\ell}$ are fixed independently of $I$ and $s(I)$, and each $V_{e,I}^{\ell}$ is one of these types: $$F_{e,s(I)}\|_I,\qquad P_{e,I}F_{e,s(I)},\qquad Z_{e,I}.$$

For every $(x,t)\\in\\operatorname{supp}w_I$ and $j\\in\\{0,1,2,3\\}$, $x+jt\\in I$ and $\\operatorname{dist}(x+jt,\\partial I)\\geq c_0r$.\n\n2. | id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-orbit-block-oscillation | Integrated sum of maximal smooth-orbit block oscillations | ≤ | $C_\varphi\sqrt w$ | The constant depends on the profile and is independent of the system, inputs, block count, and endpoints. |
| q-local-root-bound | Absolute length-weighted local-form sum | ≤ | $C_{\mathrm{loc}}(c_0,C_0)\|J\|$ | Only the admissibility parameters determine the constant. |
| q-kernel-interior-margin | Interior margin parameter | = | $1/8$ | The margin is fixed; the dyadic dilation and derivative constant depend on the profile. |
| q-input-overlap-variation | Sum of squared localized endpoint differences | ≤ | $5\mathfrak m\|E\|$ | The overlap multiplicity counts traversed schedule edges; the bound is independent of block and depth counts. |
| q-row-maximal-constant | Catalog row maximal constant | = | $\frac83\sqrt{9+L_D^2}$ | The constant depends on the catalog budget through the displayed logarithmic depth parameter. |
| q-row-logarithmic-budget | Catalog row maximal constant | ≤ | $\frac{40}{3}\log_2(2D)$ | The bound is logarithmic in the catalog budget and does not count the number of rounds. |
| q-depth-row-output | Hilbert sum norm of row outputs on nondecreasing input reads | ≤ | $\mathfrak C_D B\,\|R\|^{1/2}$ | The bound uses the joint Bessel norm and catalog budget, independently of row, depth, and block counts. |
| q-projection-delay-output | Hilbert sum norm of delayed projection differences | ≤ | $L\mathfrak C_D\|R\|^{1/2}$ | Only reads with both projection endpoints in range are retained. |
| q-smooth-row-output | Hilbert sum norm of smooth mean-zero row outputs | ≤ | $760(B_H+A)\bigl(\log_2(2d)\bigr)^2\|R\|^{1/2}$ | The carrier count determines the logarithmic budget; no Bessel inequality for a larger array is assumed. |
| q-trial-first-excess-length | Total length of first-excess request cells | ≤ | $C T^{-1}\rho^{-2}\log^2(2D_T)\|R\|$ | The bound depends on the cap, detection moment, and completed catalog budget. |
| q-delayed-replacement-error-factor | Aggregate delayed predictor replacement error | ≤ | $C\bigl(2^{-K_0}+(1+K_0)\sqrt\epsilon\bigr) \operatorname{poly}\bigl(D_{\rm pred},\log(2D_{\rm h}),1+L\bigr)\|K\|$ | The bound uses the predictor budget, logarithmic high catalog budget, energy parameter, delay, and private weight. |
| q-certified-endpoint-square-variation | Certified sum of squared endpoint-output differences | ≤ | $aG_0\|R\|$ | The group-indexed estimate requires certificates; the shared estimate uses a union across uses without a group sum. |
| q-frozen-mode-square-energy | Square energy of frozen composed mode rows | ≤ | $G_0\|R\|$ | The bound is uniform in endpoint depth, endpoint repetition, and prospective offsets. |
| q-segment-quality-gain | Truncated active-pattern contribution on a full segment | ≤ | $C_q\eta^{c_*}\|R_0\|$ | The starting accuracy is increased after the exponent choices; the fixed-input uniform local constant is included in the coefficient. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-input-size-and-variation** [claim](#stmt-lem-depth-input-size) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-depth-input-size): Pointwise triangle inequalities, charging traversed edges, and integration. -> Uniform input sizes and square-variation bounds preserved by nondecreasing restrictions.
- **step-private-catalog-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-row-catalog) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-row-catalog): Use private-coordinate orthogonality for the lower comparison and the path count for the upper comparison. -> Unique coefficient representations and unchanged catalog bounds on subroots.
- **step-row-maximal** [claim](#stmt-lem-row-maximal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-row-maximal): Assign whole rows to coefficient-threshold buckets, estimate paths with many hits, and apply binary prefix decomposition. -> Weak maximal control and its integrated maximal bound with logarithmic catalog loss.
- **step-depth-row-evaluation** [claim](#stmt-lem-row-depth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-row-depth): Apply Abel summation inside each input block, then use duality and Cauchy–Schwarz across blocks. -> A row-output square-sum bound independent of the number of input depths and blocks.
- **step-projection-increments** [claim](#stmt-cor-row-projections) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-row-projections): Split nested projection increments into support cells and retain orthogonality before applying varying-input row evaluation. -> Square-sum bounds for disjoint increments, fixed offsets, and rolling delays.
- **step-smooth-carrier-rows** [claim](#stmt-lem-smooth-depth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-smooth-depth): Expand mean-zero smooth bumps into dyadic differences, adjoin private carrier directions, and separate support depths into residue classes. -> A depth-input estimate for the selected smooth rows.
- **step-capped-trials** [claim](#stmt-lem-hbase-trial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hbase-trial): Freeze the completed rows, sum disjoint energy charges, and estimate first-excess cells through their path count. -> A cut-length bound permitting contracting restarts and cyclic adaptive fits.
- **step-earliest-increment-expansion** [claim](#stmt-lem-hbase-x-output) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hbase-x-output): Expand each slot into cyclic fitted increments and terminal residuals, grouping terms by the earliest distinct-slot updates. -> The increment tuple expansion, vanishing terminal remainder, and later-level pair smallness.
- **step-separated-scale-estimate** [claim](#stmt-lem-hbase-scale) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hbase-scale): Use the companion scalar separation and frequency analysis, bounding scheduled raw rows, compressed projected rows, and energy constituents separately. -> A decaying scale-band bound uniform over the allowed scheduled opponents.
- **step-fixed-predictor-list** [claim](#stmt-cor-hbase-predictor-list) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-hbase-predictor-list): Choose scalar shell, band, and tail cutoffs before the opponents, and retain the bounded-height surviving entries. -> A fixed finite predictor list and its actual aggregate error identity on each permitted subroot.
- **step-delayed-high-replacement** [claim](#stmt-lem-hbase-delayed) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hbase-delayed): Telescope conditional means and projection increments while fixing the original use-depth operand throughout the finer evaluations. -> Delayed replacement error control and an admissible whole rolling-energy opponent for sequential replacement.
- **step-base-active-reduction** [claim](#stmt-prop-hbase-data) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-hbase-data): Construct sequential high fits, split the replacement tuple into current and rolling parts, and remove inactive scalar triples using the companion active-scale bounds. -> Base data with rolling energies and a truncated target for the active patterns.
- **step-certified-variation** [claim](#stmt-lem-h-certified-variation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-h-certified-variation): Separate operator changes from input changes, obtain ordinary Bessel estimates first, and then evaluate on the varying reads. -> Certified variation for ball outputs and separately counted shared histories.
- **step-transfer-endpoint-variation** [claim](#stmt-cor-h-transfer-endpoint-variation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-h-transfer-endpoint-variation): Apply the endpoint calculation to the exact remaining histories after outgoing positive filters are deleted. -> Variation bounds for the complementary endpoints and their band differences used by filter transfer.
- **step-frozen-mode-rows** [claim](#stmt-lem-h-frozen-composition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-h-frozen-composition): Compose mode projections and fixed lifts into ordinary Bessel rows, insert the main histories and basic projections, and finally evaluate at endpoint reads. -> Frozen composition energy with main-catalog control at the endpoint observation depth.
- **step-complete-depth-local-estimate** [claim](#stmt-thm-depth-local) / [proof](#pm-h-37): Combine the companion localization, core counts, side stacks, and short-block comparison with the new endpoint estimates; pack restart roots and sum the accuracy expansion. -> The uniform local estimate for all prescribed depth schedules.
- **step-compact-bump** [claim](#stmt-lem-ergodic-bump) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ergodic-bump): Use the exponential compact bump, complex Cauchy estimates, and normalization by its positive integral. -> A nonnegative smooth compact bump with the required derivative class.
- **step-admissible-scale-kernel** [claim](#stmt-lem-transfer-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transfer-kernel): Take a compact antiderivative of the scale difference, multiply by a spatial bump, and differentiate in the progression cancellation directions. -> An admissible kernel whose normalized spatial integral is the exact smooth-scale difference.
- **step-continuous-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-transfer-continuous) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transfer-continuous): Average translations of a common dyadic lattice, reversing scale order to spatial depth without changing block variation. -> The continuous scale-difference test.
- **step-discrete-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-transfer-discrete) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transfer-discrete): Plant integer values on disjoint short intervals, identify progression centers exactly, and sum the smooth-kernel sampling errors. -> The integer-sequence scale-difference test.
- **step-orbit-oscillation** [claim](#stmt-prop-orbit-oscillation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transfer-discrete-2): Select maximizing prefixes and complex phases, telescope the differences, and transfer the finite-sequence estimate along increasingly long padded orbit segments. -> The uniform finite-block orbit oscillation bound.
- **step-oscillation-cauchy** [claim](#stmt-lem-ergodic-cauchy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ergodic-cauchy): Assume positive-measure nonconvergence and recursively choose deterministic blocks with a uniformly positive integral oscillation. -> Convergence on a measurable conull set.
- **step-hilbert-van-der-corput** [claim](#stmt-lem-ergodic-vdc) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ergodic-vdc): Apply the finite vector inequality, take the averaging-length limit at fixed lag range, and then increase the lag range. -> Norm convergence of the vector averages to zero.
- **step-distinct-slope-norm-induction** [claim](#stmt-lem-ergodic-cesaro) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ergodic-cesaro): Induct on the number of slopes, shift correlations to reduce the slope list, use mixing on centered factors, and expand general functions into centered terms. -> The product-valued norm limit for every finite slope list.
- **step-smooth-norm-identification** [claim](#stmt-lem-ergodic-smooth-norm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ergodic-smooth-norm): Apply the norm induction in both time directions and use vector-valued summation by parts and Riemann sums. -> The same product limit for smooth weighted sums.
- **step-signed-moment-correction** [claim](#stmt-lem-ergodic-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ergodic-moments): Smooth the interval indicator and solve the fixed Vandermonde-type moment system using broad translated bumps. -> An averaging profile arbitrarily close to the interval weight.
- **step-all-lengths-pointwise-limit** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ergodic-moments-2): Obtain smooth pointwise limits from oscillation, identify them by norm convergence and Fatou’s lemma, and recover all integer lengths through signed approximation and finite rational grids. -> The almost-everywhere product limit through all positive integer averaging lengths.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-pointwise-product-limit**: For each fixed triple of bounded measurable observables on an invertible mixing probability preserving system with measurable inverse, the progression averages converge almost everywhere to the product of their integrals. The exceptional set may depend on the observables.
- **invariant-deterministic-block-oscillation**: Smooth orbit averages have an integrated block oscillation bound growing with the square root of the number of blocks. Its constant is independent of the probability preserving system, normalized inputs, and deterministic endpoints; mixing is unnecessary for this estimate.
- **invariant-depth-local-uniformity**: The absolute length-weighted sum of admissible local forms is bounded by the root length with a constant depending only on kernel admissibility. Each input schedule must satisfy its own deterministic block size and internal variation normalization.
- **invariant-row-read-chronology**: The joint square norm of catalog rows evaluated on normalized depth inputs has a bound independent of the number of rows, depths, and input blocks. Rows in a round share an input, and their read indices are nondecreasing between rounds.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-product-limit-without-system-hypotheses**: Claim product factorization for an irreversible process or an arbitrary stationary process. -> The main theorem does not supply that conclusion when invertibility, measurable inverse, measure preservation, or mixing has not been established.
- **anti-finite-horizon-certificate**: Turn almost everywhere convergence into a certified finite stopping length or a uniform convergence rate. -> The stated conclusion is asymptotic and permits an observable-dependent exceptional set; it supplies neither a finite stopping rule nor a uniform pointwise error estimate.
- **anti-adaptive-oscillation-endpoints**: Choose the block endpoints separately at each orbit point and apply the integrated oscillation estimate unchanged. -> The endpoint quantifier covers deterministic lists. Point-dependent maximizing indices inside those fixed blocks do not authorize point-dependent block boundaries.
- **anti-bessel-bound-with-arbitrary-varying-inputs**: Evaluate every Bessel row on an unrelated input and retain the ordinary joint operator bound. -> The depth-varying estimate additionally requires normalized schedule variation, common reads within rounds, ordered reads, and the catalog and partition hypotheses.
- **anti-transfer-full-tuple-quality-to-patterns**: Assign the full replacement tuple's pair bound or pointwise quality bound to each separated active pattern. -> The reduction preserves a minimum for each pattern after using the full tuple bound. It does not establish the same pair estimate for individual patterns.
- **anti-parent-energy-as-child-energy**: Restart on a subroot by assigning it a proportional share of an arbitrary parent energy bound. -> An integrated parent estimate does not establish the required bound on each child. The proof renews the constructions and estimates on the restricted normalized schedule.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The central conclusion is almost everywhere convergence on arbitrary probability spaces for unbounded averaging lengths, rather than a finite computational construction with a decidable output contract. The source explicitly states: "The exceptional null set may depend on the triple of functions." Its proof passes through oscillation contradiction, limiting norm arguments, countable intersections of conull sets, and approximation limits. Finite arithmetic checks of selected identities would not decide this pointwise convergence theorem or certify its analytic dependencies.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The orbit theorem establishes almost everywhere convergence for measurable observables. It supplies no software input representation, executable evaluation procedure, or finite stopping contract. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The smooth-weight limit assumes an invertible probability preserving mixing system. A simulated trajectory requires a separate justification of those assumptions and a separate analysis of finite observation error. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The oscillation criterion proves convergence by excluding persistent oscillation on a set of positive measure. It does not construct a diagnostic that decides convergence from a finite observed prefix. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The capped insertion trial gives a mathematical stopping and packing argument conditional on successful requests, charged projection rows, and a completed catalog budget. It does not supply an effective request oracle or a computational complexity contract. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The persistent catalog is a system of Hilbert-valued columns with private coordinates and unique constant coefficients. Its comparison bounds do not specify a storage representation, update implementation, or query cost. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The row maximal estimate controls function norms using a catalog budget and a joint Bessel inequality. These quantities have no supplied correspondence to processor parallelism, device memory, or accelerator throughput. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Convergence of multiple correlations under mixing is an asymptotic statement about a measure preserving system. It supplies no adversary model, computational indistinguishability claim, or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The smooth-row estimate concerns fixed bounded carriers, mean-zero test functions, and normalized depth inputs. It provides no learning rule, training objective, or generalization guarantee for learned features. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The inherited projections yield energy bounds for ordered increments on normalized schedules. Their role is analytic control; the result does not define an optimization objective, feasibility problem, or approximation guarantee. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The depth-varying row estimate requires a common nondecreasing read schedule and spatial partition refinement. It does not model independently scheduled replicas, communication failures, or distributed agreement. |
| other (Harmonic analysis and ergodic theory) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The paper constructs admissible local kernels realizing adjacent smooth scale differences and bounds local multilinear forms for inputs with controlled variation in depth. These are concrete analytic constructions supporting the orbit convergence argument. |

          "anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(X,\\mathcal F,\\mu)$ be an arbitrary probability space and let $T:X\\to X$ be an invertible mixing probability preserving transformation with measurable inverse. For every triple of bounded measurable functions $f_1,f_2,f_3:X\\to\\mathbb C$, $$A_N(f_1,f_2,f_3)(x)\n \\longrightarrow \\prod_{j=1}^3\\int_X f_j\\,d\\mu\n \\quad\\text{for $\\mu$-almost every }x$$ as $N\\to\\infty$ through all positive integers. The exceptional null set may depend on the triple of functions.",
          "source_object": "stmt-thm-main"
        }
      ],
      "space_complexity": null,
      "strength": "none",
      "time_complexity": null,
      "verification": "PENDING",
      "why": "The orbit theorem establishes almost everywhere convergence for measurable observables. It supplies no software input representation, executable evaluation procedure, or finite stopping contract."
    },

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-ergodic-smooth-norm",
          "exact_quote": "On every invertible probability preserving system satisfying <a href=\"#eq:mixing\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:mixing\">[eq:mixing]</a>, fix an integer $r\\geq1$, pairwise distinct nonzero integers $\\lambda_1,\\ldots,\\lambda_r$, and bounded measurable $f_1,\\ldots,f_r:X\\to\\mathbb C$. Set $$F_n=\\prod_{j=1}^r f_j(T^{\\lambda_jn}\\,\\cdot),\\qquad\n p=\\prod_{j=1}^r\\int_Xf_j\\,d\\mu,\\qquad n\\in\\mathbb Z.$$ For every $\\varphi\\in C_c^1(\\mathbb R;\\mathbb C)$ with $\\int_{\\mathbb R}\\varphi=1$, $$\\left\\|\\sum_{n\\in\\mathbb Z}a^{-1}\\varphi(n/a)F_n-p\\right\\|_2\n       \\longrightarrow0\\qquad(a\\to\\infty,\\ a\\geq1).$$ In particular the conclusion holds for every averaging profile.",
          "source_object": "stmt-lem-ergodic-smooth-norm"
        }
      ],

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-ergodic-cauchy",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(X,\\mathcal F,\\mu)$ be any probability space and let $Z_k:X\\to\\mathbb C$, $k\\geq0$, be measurable. Suppose that $|Z_k(x)|\\leq M<\\infty$ for every $k,x$, and that there is $C<\\infty$ such that, for every integer $w\\geq1$ and every deterministic list $0\\leq k_1<\\cdots<k_{w+1}$, $$\\sum_{i=1}^w\\int_X\n   \\max_{k_i\\leq\\ell\\leq k_{i+1}}|Z_\\ell-Z_{k_i}|\\,d\\mu\n       \\leq C\\sqrt w.$$ Then $Z_k(x)$ converges in $\\mathbb C$ on a measurable conull set.",
          "source_object": "stmt-lem-ergodic-cauchy"
        }
      ],

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-hbase-trial",
          "exact_quote": "Let $R$ be an attempt root with a restricted normalized input schedule $F_s$. Let the candidate request cells belong to a fixed finite original request tree below $R$. Fix $0<\\rho\\le1$, $D_0\\ge2$, and an integer cap $T\\ge1$. Process candidates in nondecreasing request-cell depth, allowing repetitions at one cell. Stop before the first request that would exceed $T$ successful requests on its spatial path, and remove that cut cell and its candidate descendants from the trial.*\n\n\n*For each successful request $v$ at $I_v$, suppose there is a set $\\mathcal R_v$ of charged row indices, with these sets pairwise disjoint, such that $$\\sum_{\\nu\\in\\mathcal R_v}\n       \\|\\Delta_\\nu F_{s(\\nu)}\\|_2^2\n \\ge c\\rho^2|I_v|.$$ Assume all charged rows are a Bessel-norm-one subfamily of one realized finite nested sequence of full-partition projections and satisfy every hypothesis of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:row-depth\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:row-depth\">18</a>. Suppose its completed catalog has budget $$D_T\\le C D_0(T+1)^C,$$ where $D_0$ includes all predetermined columns, including their future admissions on this attempt. Then the disjoint first-excess cells $\\mathcal C_T$, which are original request-tree cells, obey $$\\sum_{I\\in\\mathcal C_T}|I|\n \\le C T^{-1}\\rho^{-2}\\log^2(2D_T)|R|.                        \\label{eq:hbase-trial-cut}$$ If $$a=1+\\log(2D_0)+\\log(1/\\rho),\\qquad\n T=\\left\\lceil C_*\\rho^{-4}(1+a)^8\\right\\rceil,$$ then a sufficiently large fixed $C_*$ makes the right side at most $|R|/4$, and $\\log(2T)\\le C(a+\\log C_*)$.",
          "source_object": "stmt-lem-hbase-trial"
        }
      ],
      "space_complexity": null,
      "strength": "none",
      "time_complexity": null,
      "verification": "PENDING",
      "why": "The capped insertion trial gives a mathematical stopping and packing argument conditional on successful requests, charged projection rows, and a completed catalog budget. It does not supply an effective request oracle or a computational complexity contract."
    },

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-row-catalog",
          "exact_quote": "For every dyadic cell $Q\\subset R$ and every vector $a=(a_v)_{v\\in\\mathcal V(Q)}$, one has $$D^{-1}\\|a\\|_{\\ell^2}\n \\le \\left\\|\\sum_{v\\in\\mathcal V(Q)}a_v\\Phi_v(y)\\right\\|_{\\mathcal H}\n \\le D^{3/2}\\|a\\|_{\\ell^2}\n \\quad\\hbox{for almost every }y\\in Q.\n \\label{eq:row-catalog-comparison}$$ In particular constant coefficients in such a representation are unique. Coefficients may be regarded as vectors in the one space $\\ell^2(\\mathcal V)$ by extending them by zero; the vector for a combination on $Q$ is unchanged when the combination is restricted to a descendant.*\n\n*These assertions and the same value of $D$ hold after restriction to a dyadic subroot $R'\\subset R$: discard birth cells disjoint from $R'$, give an inherited label with $R'\\subset J_v$ the new birth cell $R'$, and retain the other birth cells contained in $R'$.",
          "source_object": "stmt-lem-row-catalog"
        }
      ],
      "space_complexity": null,
      "strength": "none",
      "time_complexity": null,

      "why": "The persistent catalog is a system of Hilbert-valued columns with private coordinates and unique constant coefficients. Its comparison bounds do not specify a storage representation, update implementation, or query cost."

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-row-maximal",
          "exact_quote": "Use a root and catalog satisfying Equation <a href=\"#eq:row-catalog-budget\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:row-catalog-budget\">[eq:row-catalog-budget]</a>. Let $N\\ge1$, and for $1\\le n\\le N$ let $\\pi_n$ and $\\sigma_n$ be finite dyadic partitions of $R$ such that $$\\sigma_n\\text{ refines }\\pi_n,\\qquad\n \\pi_{n+1}\\text{ refines }\\sigma_n\\quad(1\\le n<N).$$ Let $\\mathcal R_1,\\ldots,\\mathcal R_N$ be pairwise disjoint finite row index sets, with union $\\mathcal R$. For every $\\nu\\in\\mathcal R_n$, let $\\mathcal Y_\\nu$ be a Hilbert space, let $Q_\\nu\\in\\pi_n$, and let $A_\\nu:L^2(R;\\mathcal H)\\to\\mathcal Y_\\nu$ be bounded and linear. Assume the following two properties.*\n\n1.  *The whole row has support $Q_\\nu$: $A_\\nu=A_\\nu\\mathbf1_{Q_\\nu}$. For every $K\\in\\sigma_n$ and every $c\\in\\mathcal Y_\\nu$, there are spatially constant coefficients $a_{\\nu,K,v}(c)$ such that, in $L^2(R;\\mathcal H)$, $$\\mathbf1_K A_\\nu^*c\n           =\\mathbf1_K\\sum_{v\\in\\mathcal V(K)}\n                            a_{\\nu,K,v}(c)\\Phi_v.$$ There is no asserted uniform bound on these coefficients.*\n\n2.  *For some $B\\ge0$ the original rows satisfy the joint operator estimate $$\\sum_{\\nu\\in\\mathcal R}\\|A_\\nu z\\|_{\\mathcal Y_\\nu}^2\n           \\le B^2\\|z\\|_{L^2(R;\\mathcal H)}^2\n           \\qquad(z\\in L^2(R;\\mathcal H)).$$*\n\n\n*Cells with no rows and empty rounds are allowed, as are multiple rows with the same support cell. Define $$L_D=1+\\lceil5\\log_2D\\rceil,\\qquad\n \\mathfrak C_D=\\frac83\\sqrt{9+L_D^2}\n \\le \\frac{40}{3}\\log_2(2D).$$ For $1\\le p\\le q\\le N$ and any tests $c_\\nu\\in\\mathcal Y_\\nu$ in these rounds, put $$\\begin{split}\n E_c&=\\sum_{n=p}^q\\sum_{\\nu\\in\\mathcal R_n}\\|c_\\nu\\|_{\\mathcal Y_\\nu}^2,\\\\\n g_n&=\\sum_{\\nu\\in\\mathcal R_n}A_\\nu^*c_\\nu,\\qquad\n S_j=\\sum_{n=p}^j g_n,\\quad S_{p-1}=0,\\\\\n M(y)&=\\max_{p-1\\le j\\le q}\\|S_j(y)\\|_{\\mathcal H}.\n \\end{split}$$ Then, for every $\\lambda>0$, $$|\\{y\\in R:M(y)>\\lambda\\}|\n \\le \\min\\left\\{|R|,\n       \\frac{\\mathfrak C_D^2 B^2 E_c}{4\\lambda^2}\\right\\},\n \\qquad\n \\|M\\|_{L^1(R)}\n \\le \\mathfrak C_D B\\,|R|^{1/2}E_c^{1/2}.\n \\label{eq:row-maximal}$$",
          "source_object": "stmt-lem-row-maximal"
        }
      ],
      "space_complexity": null,
      "strength": "none",
      "time_complexity": null,
      "verification": "PENDING",
      "why": "The row maximal estimate controls function norms using a catalog budget and a joint Bessel inequality. These quantities have no supplied correspondence to processor parallelism, device memory, or accelerator throughput."

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-smooth-depth",
          "exact_quote": "Let $R$ be a finite dyadic root and let $\\mathcal N$ be a finite set of carrier labels. For each $\\nu\\in\\mathcal N$ fix one measurable function $m_\\nu:R\\to\\mathbb C$ with $\\|m_\\nu\\|_\\infty\\le1$. This function is the same for every interval using the label $\\nu$. Let $\\mathcal E$ be a finite set of pairs $(I,\\nu)$, where $I\\subset R$ is a dyadic interval of depth $s(I)$ and $\\nu\\in\\mathcal N$. Thus at most one row is selected for a given pair $(I,\\nu)$.*\n\n\n*For each selected pair, let $\\omega_{I,\\nu}\\in C^1(\\overline I)$ satisfy $$\\int_I\\omega_{I,\\nu}(y)\\,dy=0,\\qquad\n \\|\\omega_{I,\\nu}\\|_\\infty+\n       |I|\\|\\omega_{I,\\nu}'\\|_\\infty\\le A$$ for one $A\\ge0$. Suppose the selected physical rows $$\\mathcal W_{I,\\nu}f\n   =|I|^{-1/2}\\int_I m_\\nu(y)\\omega_{I,\\nu}(y)f(y)\\,dy$$ satisfy, for one $B_H\\ge0$, $$\\sum_{(I,\\nu)\\in\\mathcal E}|\\mathcal W_{I,\\nu}f|^2\n \\le B_H^2\\|f\\|_{L^2(R)}^2\\qquad(f\\in L^2(R)).$$ Let the scalar functions $(f_s)_{s\\in\\mathsf S}$ satisfy Equation <a href=\"#eq:row-input-normalization\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:row-input-normalization\">[eq:row-input-normalization]</a>, with absolute values in place of Hilbert norms, and assume that every $s(I)$ belongs to $\\mathsf S$. Put $$d=2+\\#\\mathcal N,\\qquad h_0=\\lceil10\\log_2(2d)\\rceil.$$ Then $$\\begin{split}\n \\left(\\sum_{(I,\\nu)\\in\\mathcal E}\n           |\\mathcal W_{I,\\nu}f_{s(I)}|^2\\right)^{1/2}\n &\\le \\mathfrak C_d\\left[\n       (h_0+1)(B_H+3A)\n       +2A\\sqrt d\\,(h_0+3)2^{-h_0}\\right]|R|^{1/2}\\\\\n &\\le 760(B_H+A)\\bigl(\\log_2(2d)\\bigr)^2|R|^{1/2}.\n \\end{split}\n \\label{eq:smooth-depth}$$ The estimate uses only the displayed Bessel inequality for the selected set $\\mathcal E$, not for any larger array of intervals and carriers.",
          "source_object": "stmt-lem-smooth-depth"
        }
      ],

          "anchor": "stmt-cor-row-projections",
          "exact_quote": "Use the catalog of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:row-catalog\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:row-catalog\">16</a>. On a finite interval of integers $h\\ge s_R$, let $\\mathcal P_h$ be the full partition of $R$ into cells of depth $h$. On each $Q\\in\\mathcal P_h$, let $P_{h,Q}$ be the orthogonal projection in $L^2(Q;\\mathcal H)$ onto constant-coefficient combinations of a subset $\\mathcal M_h(Q)\\subset\\mathcal V(Q)$. Assume inheritance: if $Q'\\in\\mathcal P_{h+1}$ is contained in $Q$, then $\\mathcal M_h(Q)\\subset\\mathcal M_{h+1}(Q')$. Let $P_h$ be the direct sum of the $P_{h,Q}$ on $L^2(R;\\mathcal H)$.*\n\n\n*Suppose $(F_s)_{s\\in\\mathsf S}$ satisfies Equation <a href=\"#eq:row-input-normalization\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:row-input-normalization\">[eq:row-input-normalization]</a>. For any integer $J\\ge1$ and $$r_1\\le t_1\\le r_2\\le t_2\\le\\cdots\\le r_J\\le t_J$$ in the projection depth range and nondecreasing $s_1,\\ldots,s_J\\in\\mathsf S$, one has $$\\sum_{j=1}^J\\|(P_{t_j}-P_{r_j})F_{s_j}\\|_2^2\n \\le \\mathfrak C_D^2|R|.\n \\label{eq:row-disjoint-increments}$$ If the intervals can be divided into $\\mathfrak m$ such ordered families whose reads are nondecreasing, the right side is multiplied by $\\mathfrak m$.*\n\n\n\n*For each $s\\in\\mathsf S$ with $s\\ge s_R$, let $\\mathcal U_s$ be any subcollection of the depth-$s$ cells of $R$. In particular fix an integer $\\ell$ and retain only those $s$ for which $s+\\ell$ and $s+\\ell+1$ are in the projection range. Then $$\\sum_s\\sum_{I\\in\\mathcal U_s}|I|\\,\n       \\|(P_{s+\\ell+1}-P_{s+\\ell})F_s\\|_{2,I}^2\n \\le \\mathfrak C_D^2|R|.\n \\label{eq:row-increment}$$ For every integer $L\\ge0$, retaining only $s\\in\\mathsf S$ with $s\\ge s_R$ for which $s$ and $s+L$ are in the projection range, $$\\left(\\sum_s\\sum_{I\\in\\mathcal U_s}|I|\\,\n       \\|(P_{s+L}-P_s)F_s\\|_{2,I}^2\\right)^{1/2}\n \\le L\\mathfrak C_D|R|^{1/2}\n \\le (1+L)\\mathfrak C_D|R|^{1/2}.\n \\label{eq:row-delay}$$",
          "source_object": "stmt-cor-row-projections"
        }
      ],

          "exact_quote": "Let the root, catalog, partitions, and rows satisfy every hypothesis of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:row-maximal\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:row-maximal\">17</a>. Then $$\\left(\\sum_{n=1}^N\\sum_{\\nu\\in\\mathcal R_n}\n          \\|A_\\nu F_{s_n}\\|_{\\mathcal Y_\\nu}^2\\right)^{1/2}\n \\le \\mathfrak C_D B\\,|R|^{1/2}\n \\le \\frac{40}{3} B\\log_2(2D)\\,|R|^{1/2}.\n \\label{eq:row-depth}$$ The constant is independent of the number of rows, depths, and input blocks.",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-def-averaging-profile",
          "exact_quote": "An averaging profile is a real function $\\varphi\\in C_c^\\infty(\\mathbb R;\\mathbb R)$ such that, for some $G\\geq1$, $$\\|\\varphi^{(n)}\\|_\\infty\n   \\leq\\bigl(G(n+2)\\bigr)^{G(n+2)}\n       \\qquad(n=0,1,2,\\ldots),\n \\label{eq:profile-derivatives}$$ and $$\\int_{\\mathbb R}\\varphi(t)\\,dt=1,\\qquad\n \\int_{\\mathbb R}t^j\\varphi(t)\\,dt=0\\quad(1\\leq j\\leq3).\n \\label{eq:profile-moments}$$ The function may have either sign. For $a>0$, write $\\varphi_a(t)=a^{-1}\\varphi(t/a)$.",
          "source_object": "stmt-def-averaging-profile"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-def-transfer-local-kernels",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $c_0>0$ and $C_0\\geq1$. For an interval $I$ of length $r=|I|$, a kernel $w_I\\in C_c^\\infty(\\mathbb R^2;\\mathbb C)$ is admissible if the following hold.\n\n1.  For every $(x,t)\\in\\operatorname{supp}w_I$ and $j\\in\\{0,1,2,3\\}$, $x+jt\\in I$ and $\\operatorname{dist}(x+jt,\\partial I)\\geq c_0r$.\n\n2.  For every $v\\in\\mathbb R$ and $j=0,1,2,3$, $$\\int_{\\mathbb R}w_I(v-jt,t)\\,dt=0.$$\n\n3.  For all nonnegative integers $p,q$, with $n=p+q$, $$\\|\\partial_x^p\\partial_t^q w_I\\|_\\infty\n         \\leq r^{-n}\\bigl(C_0(n+2)\\bigr)^{C_0(n+2)}.$$\n\nFor measurable scalar functions $z_j:\\mathbb R\\to\\mathbb C$, define the associated local form, whenever its integral is finite, by $$H_I(z_0,z_1,z_2,z_3)\n   =r^{-2}\\int_{\\mathbb R^2}w_I(x,t)\n                  \\prod_{j=0}^3z_j(x+jt)\\,dx\\,dt.$$ For a Hilbert-valued argument with a distinguished physical scalar coordinate, the form uses that coordinate. We write $\\|z\\|_{p,I}=(|I|^{-1}\\int_I\\|z\\|^p)^{1/p}$ for the normalized interval norm, with the usual essential supremum when $p=\\infty$. Norms without an interval subscript use ordinary Lebesgue measure.",
          "source_object": "stmt-def-transfer-local-kernels"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-transfer-kernel",
          "exact_quote": "For every averaging profile $\\varphi$ in Definition <a href=\"#def:averaging-profile\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:averaging-profile\">2</a>, there are a dyadic integer $D_\\varphi=2^d$, $d\\in\\mathbb Z_{\\geq0}$, a constant $C_{0,\\varphi}\\geq1$, and $\\mathcal W_\\varphi\\in C_c^\\infty(\\mathbb R^2;\\mathbb R)$ such that the following holds. For every $a>0$ and every interval $I$ of length $r=D_\\varphi a$, with midpoint $m_I$, the kernel $$w_I(x,t)=\\mathcal W_\\varphi\\left(\\frac{x-m_I}{r},\\frac{t}{r}\\right)$$ is admissible with $c_0=1/8$ and $C_0=C_{0,\\varphi}$. Moreover, for every $t\\in\\mathbb R$, $$\\frac1r\\int_{\\mathbb R}\\mathcal W_\\varphi(z,t/r)\\,dz\n    =\\varphi_{2a}(t)-\\varphi_a(t).\n \\label{eq:transfer-kernel-normalization}$$ The parameters are fixed once $\\varphi$ is fixed.",
          "source_object": "stmt-lem-transfer-kernel"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-depth-local",
          "exact_quote": "Fix the admissibility parameters $c_0,C_0$. Let $J$ be an interval in any dyadic lattice, let $\\mathcal T$ be a finite rooted tree below $J$, and let $d$ be its largest depth in $J$. Let $\\mathcal I\\subseteq\\mathcal T$, with an admissible kernel on each of its intervals. For each $j\\in\\{0,1,2,3\\}$, let $f_{j,s}:J\\to\\mathbb C$, $0\\le s\\le d$, be measurable, and write $f_j=(f_{j,s})_{s=0}^d$. Suppose that $\\{0,\\ldots,d\\}$ is partitioned into $W_j\\ge1$ deterministic nonempty consecutive blocks and that, on every block $B$, $$\\mathcal V_B(f_j)(y)\\le W_j^{-1/2}\n \\quad\\text{for almost every }y\\in J.$$ The four partitions may differ. Extend the inputs by zero outside $J$. Then $$\\sum_{I\\in\\mathcal I}|I|\n  \\bigl|H_I(f_{0,s(I)},f_{1,s(I)},f_{2,s(I)},f_{3,s(I)})\\bigr|\n \\le C_{\\mathrm{loc}}(c_0,C_0)|J|.\n \\label{eq:depth-local}$$ The constant is independent of the lattice, root, tree, kernels, depth range, blocks, and their numbers.",
          "source_object": "stmt-thm-depth-local"
        }
      ],
      "space_complexity": null,
      "strength": "strong",
      "time_complexity": null,
      "verification": "PENDING",
      "why": "The paper constructs admissible local kernels realizing adjacent smooth scale differences and bounds local multilinear forms for inputs with controlled variation in depth. These are concrete analytic constructions supporting the orbit convergence argument."
    }
  ],
  "generation_status": "COMPLETE",
  "pm_id": "PM-154.4",
  "schema": "pm.domains.v2"
}

## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-pointwise-system-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The pointwise product limit requires invertibility, mixing, probability preservation, and a measurable inverse. Blocked use: ['Apply the product-limit theorem to an irreversible transition rule or a process whose mixing property has not been established.']
- **limit-bounded-measurable-observables** (hypothesis): The main theorem concerns a fixed triple of bounded measurable complex functions. Blocked use: ['Extend the pointwise conclusion to unbounded observables without an additional argument.']
- **limit-observable-dependent-exceptional-set** (formal_scope): The exceptional null set may depend on the chosen triple; a common convergence set for all bounded observables is not supplied. Blocked use: ['Claim simultaneous convergence for every observable triple outside one universal exceptional set.']
- **limit-pointwise-slope-scope** (formal_scope): The main pointwise conclusion concerns the specified triple progression. The distinct-slope lemma establishes convergence in norm and does not itself establish the corresponding pointwise conclusion. Blocked use: ['Infer pointwise convergence for arbitrary distinct integer slopes solely from the norm-convergence lemma.', 'Extend the main theorem directly to additional observables or polynomial orbit times.']
- **limit-no-finite-pointwise-modulus** (unknown): A finite stopping length or quantitative pointwise convergence modulus is ABSENT from the stated theorem. The norm induction does not require a convergence rate uniform in the lag. Blocked use: ['Use asymptotic convergence as a finite-sample error certificate or an operational timeout guarantee.']
- **limit-deterministic-oscillation-blocks** (hypothesis): The oscillation estimate requires an averaging profile, normalized bounded inputs, and deterministic endpoint lists. Choosing maximizing indices inside fixed blocks does not authorize point-dependent block boundaries. Blocked use: ['Apply the same integrated bound after adapting block endpoints separately to each orbit point.', 'Identify the smooth limit with the product of integrals using the oscillation estimate alone, without mixing.']
- **limit-oscillation-and-correlation-criteria** (hypothesis): The convergence criteria require their full uniform boundedness and asymptotic hypotheses. The correlation criterion requires existence of each fixed-lag limit and decay of those limits. Blocked use: ['Certify convergence from finitely many observed oscillations.', 'Replace existence and decay of correlation limits by empirical smallness over a finite lag range.']
- **limit-signed-profile-approximation** (approximation): Averaging profiles may have either sign. Approximation of interval weights permits support and derivative constants to depend on the interval parameter and tolerance. Blocked use: ['Interpret the constructed profiles as nonnegative probability weights.', 'Assume a uniform support radius or derivative constant across increasingly accurate approximations.']
- **limit-kernel-admissibility** (hypothesis): Smoothness alone does not establish kernel admissibility. Interior progression-coordinate support, directional cancellation, and the prescribed derivative bounds are required. Blocked use: ['Substitute arbitrary smooth kernels into the local estimate.', 'Treat an approximate cancellation identity as exact without bounding the resulting error.']
- **limit-complete-normalized-depth-schedules** (hypothesis): The local estimate uses a finite rooted dyadic tree and full depth schedules with deterministic consecutive block partitions and the prescribed pointwise size-plus-variation normalization in every slot. Blocked use: ['Apply the depth-independent estimate to arbitrary depth changes controlled only by individual input sizes.', 'Leave inputs unspecified at depths without summation intervals.']
- **limit-transfer-support-and-scales** (hypothesis): The continuous and discrete transfer tests assume common bounded support and normalized block variation. The discrete test restricts scale indices to be nonnegative so its sampling errors are summable. Blocked use: ['Apply the finite-support transfer estimate unchanged to arbitrary infinite sequences.', 'Extend the discrete scale range to arbitrarily negative indices using the same error argument.']
- **limit-persistent-private-column-budget** (hypothesis): The catalog comparisons rely on persistent columns, distinct private directions, lower bounds on private weights, and the count of all birth labels on a path, including omitted labels. Blocked use: ['Merge coinciding physical columns while discarding their distinct private coordinates.', 'Count only currently selected labels when verifying the catalog budget.']
- **limit-row-chronology-and-ordinary-bessel-bound** (hypothesis): An ordinary Bessel inequality alone does not justify unrelated varying inputs. The row estimate also requires the catalog interface, support and observation refinement, normalized block variation, and nondecreasing common reads within rounds. Blocked use: ['Evaluate each row on an independently chosen input while retaining the fixed-input operator bound.', 'Reorder the reads arbitrarily or split a whole row according to a later stopping decision.']
- **limit-inherited-projection-increments** (hypothesis): Projection increment bounds require inherited nested ranges, ordered projection intervals, nondecreasing reads, and projection endpoints within the stated range. Blocked use: ['Apply the same square-sum bound to unrelated projections.', 'Ignore interval overlap or evaluate a delayed projection beyond its defined range.']
- **limit-fixed-smooth-row-carriers** (hypothesis): Each carrier is fixed across all intervals bearing its label. Smooth rows require exact mean zero, the stated value and derivative control, and an ordinary Bessel inequality for the selected array. Blocked use: ['Let a carrier vary between intervals under one label without proving a replacement estimate.', 'Assume that a Bessel inequality for the selected array also holds for an enlarged array.']
- **limit-insertion-trial-charges** (hypothesis): The capped-trial packing estimate assumes disjoint ownership of charged rows, successful-request energy gains, a realized nested projection sequence, and the completed catalog budget including future predetermined admissions. Blocked use: ['Charge the same projection increment to several successful requests.', 'Interpret the cap as an executable running-time bound without an effective request procedure.']
- **limit-fixed-predictors-and-original-operands** (hypothesis): The scalar predictor list and its coefficients are chosen before the opposing sequences and completed high catalogs. Delayed replacement retains the original use-depth operand and requires the same predictor to persist on continued descendants. Blocked use: ['Choose predictors after examining the opponents while claiming the same uniform contract.', 'Replace the original input by the input at the later projection depth.', 'Split a whole rolling-energy constituent into pieces without reestablishing admissibility.']
- **limit-full-tuple-quality-before-pattern-split** (formal_scope): The pair estimate and pointwise quality bound belong to the full replacement tuple. The active reduction retains truncated contributions for the separated patterns. Blocked use: ["Assign the full tuple's pair estimate or pointwise quality bound to each individual active pattern."]
- **limit-certified-and-shared-endpoints** (hypothesis): Group-indexed endpoint variation requires downstream actual-use certificates and complete inherited histories with coupled pad choices. Shared outputs use a union across their uses and have no group sum. Blocked use: ['Sum a shared endpoint separately for every group.', 'Apply the group-indexed estimate to uncertified endpoint ranges or newly resampled pad choices.']
- **limit-frozen-mode-availability** (hypothesis): Frozen composition requires block-independent lifts, the per-group ordinary Bessel bound, certified usable containing endpoint cells, and mode labels already available at those endpoints. Blocked use: ['Resample mode labels or lifts between freezing blocks while invoking the same composition estimate.', 'Replace the containing endpoint support by a smaller output cell when applying endpoint synthesis.']
- **limit-restart-estimates-renewed-on-subroots** (formal_scope): Restarts preserve original input depths and renew the estimates on restricted normalized schedules. An arbitrary parent energy bound does not imply proportional energy on each child. Blocked use: ['Allocate parent energy proportionally to child length without a new-root proof.', 'Reset input depth indices when restarting a localization attempt.']
- **limit-companion-machinery-and-parameter-order** (formal_scope): The depth-local proof imports analytic estimates from the companion argument and uses a common exponent chosen in its permitted parameter order before later power and starting-accuracy choices. Blocked use: ['Treat the local proof as independent of its companion analytic hypotheses.', 'Use an arbitrary exponent in the displayed range without verifying the shared degree restrictions.']
- **limit-no-finite-certificate-for-pointwise-convergence** (nonconstructive): Finite checks of selected identities do not decide almost-everywhere convergence on arbitrary probability spaces. The proof uses limiting oscillation arguments and countable intersections of convergence sets. Blocked use: ['Present agreement on finite orbit prefixes as a certificate of the full pointwise theorem.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove pointwise convergence of triple ergodic averages for every invertible mixing probability preserving transformation <i>T</i> of an arbitrary probability space $`(X,\mathcal F,\mu)`$, with measurable inverse. For every triple of bounded measurable functions $`f_1,f_2,f_3:X\to\mathbb C`$,

$`\displaystyle \frac1N\sum_{n=1}^N f_1(T^nx)f_2(T^{2n}x)f_3(T^{3n}x) \longrightarrow \prod_{j=1}^3\int_X f_j\,d\mu`$

for <i>μ</i>-almost every <i>x</i> as $`N\to\infty`$ through all positive integers.
